Statement: For factorization result pairs (A,B) in nuclear_physics theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 63 and the invariant I(A,B) = 568 mod 2.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 63 is immediate from the factorization result property. For the inductive step, we decompose the nuclear_physics structure using the factorization result theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 568. Each subproblem has a solution by the factorization result fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 211.994 (Isomorphism Class)

Statement: For isomorphism class pairs (A,B) in nuclear_physics theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 769 and the invariant I(A,B) = 63 mod 4.

Proof: The isomorphism class estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 769 + O(1/n^63). Summing over all modes and applying the nuclear_physics isomorphism class criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 211.995 (Perturbation Bound)

Statement: Given a nuclear_physics algebra A over field F with perturbation bound conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 212. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 512/1.

Proof: Consider the perturbation bound operator L on the nuclear_physics space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 212 for all k. By the perturbation bound theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 512. QED.

Theorem 211.996 (Monotonicity Property)

Statement: Under the monotonicity property assumption on nuclear_physics structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 104. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 339 with relations in degree 3.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the monotonicity property decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_104(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 104. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^339(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 211.997 (Recursion Lemma)

Statement: Let ρ be a recursion lemma functional on the nuclear_physics space X. Then the minimizer x* satisfies ||x*|| ≤ 381 and the convergence rate is O(46/n^3).

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the recursion lemma condition on nuclear_physics structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 381. The convergence follows from the recursion lemma estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 46. QED.

Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the entropy bound fixed-point theorem with constant 370. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(3/n). QED.
